(b) is proven as (a).

(c) Fix any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \Gamma _u,$$\end{document}x∈Γu, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r\in (0,\frac{\alpha _0}{\Lambda })$$\end{document}r∈(0,α0Λ) and consider the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ S:=\{y\in \mathbb {R}^2:\,\,\textrm{dist}_\varphi (y,F_u)=\textrm{dist}_\varphi (y,\Gamma _u)=r\}. $$\end{document}S:={y∈R2:distφ(y,Fu)=distφ(y,Γu)=r}.Note that SS contains two little arcs outside the strip \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I\times \mathbb {R},$$\end{document}I×R, and hence, tangent balls may have centers outside it. Observe also that for any y∈ Sy∈S the φ φ-ball \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathring{W}_r^\varphi (y)$$\end{document}W˚rφ(y) is “tangent” to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\Gamma }_u$$\end{document}Γ¯u at some point and does not intersect \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_u.$$\end{document}Fu. In view of (a) and (b), it suffices to show that there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{y}\in S$$\end{document}y¯∈S such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \partial W_r^\varphi (\bar{y}).$$\end{document}x∈∂Wrφ(y¯). Indeed, otherwise, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma _u$$\end{document}Γu is a generalized graph (an arcwise connected set), we could find y∈ Sy∈S such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Gamma _u \cap \partial W_r^\varphi (y)$$\end{document}Γu∩∂Wrφ(y) contains two distinct points p and q, and x lies in the relative interior of the subcurve of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma _u$$\end{document}Γu with endpoints p and q, but does not belong to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial W_r^\varphi (y).$$\end{document}∂Wrφ(y). However, by (a), the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\Gamma }_u \cap \partial W_r^\varphi (y)$$\end{document}Γ¯u∩∂Wrφ(y) is connected, and hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \partial W_r^\varphi (y),$$\end{document}x∈∂Wrφ(y), a contradiction.

For a similar reason, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \Gamma _u$$\end{document}x∈Γu and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r\in (0,\frac{\alpha _0}{\Lambda })$$\end{document}r∈(0,α0Λ) there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_r^\varphi (y)$$\end{document}Wrφ(y) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathring{W}_r^\varphi (y)\cap E_u=\emptyset $$\end{document}W˚rφ(y)∩Eu=∅ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \Gamma _u \cap \partial W_r^\varphi (y).$$\end{document}x∈Γu∩∂Wrφ(y). □ □

One corollary of Proposition 4.3 is the following Lipschitz regularity of u.

Corollary 4.4

Let φ φ be a partially monotone anisotropy such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^\varphi $$\end{document}Wφ does not have vertical facets and, given a bounded open interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I\subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}I⊂R and Λ >0,Λ>0, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\textrm{loc}}(I)$$\end{document}u∈BVloc(I) be a Λ Λ-local minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi $$\end{document}Aφ in I satisfying (4.1). Then u is Lipschitz continuous in I.

Proof of Theorem 4.1

By Corollary 4.4, u is Lipschitz continuous in I. Assume now φ φ is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^2,$$\end{document}C2, elliptic and partially monotone. By Proposition 4.3, the subgraph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_u:=\textrm{sg}(u)$$\end{document}Eu:=sg(u) of u satisfies uniform4 interior and exterior φ φ-ball conditions at every point of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(I\times \mathbb {R}) \cap \partial E_u.$$\end{document}(I×R)∩∂Eu. Some generalizations

In this section we relax the Λ Λ-local minimality assumption on u in Theorem 4.1. To this aim, we start with the following

Definition 4.5

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-local minimizer) Given an anisotropy φ φ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} {R}^2,$$\end{document}R2, γ >0,γ>0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\textrm{loc}}(I)\cap L^\infty (I)$$\end{document}u∈BVloc(I)∩L∞(I) is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-local minimizer provided that its subgraph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} satisfies4.17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_\varphi (E_u,\Omega ) \le P_\varphi (F,\Omega ) + \Lambda |E_u\Delta F| \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pφ(Eu,Ω)≤Pφ(F,Ω)+Λ|EuΔF|for Notice that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-local minimizers are not necessarily Λ Λ-local minimizers, as local perturbations are taken only in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I\times (-\gamma ,\gamma ).$$\end{document}I×(-γ,γ). Still, we can readily check that the density estimates in Lemma 2.9 and properties in (2.7) hold, and therefore we can speak about closed and open representatives of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_u.$$\end{document}Eu. Moreover, in case I and u are bounded, we can apply (4.17) with the set D in the proof of Proposition 4.3 provided for instance4.18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \gamma > \frac{\alpha _0\varphi (\textbf{e}_1)}{2\Lambda }; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}γ>α0φ(e1)2Λ;for such γ ,γ, if φ φ is partially monotone and u satisfies (4.10), all assertions of Proposition 4.3 are valid. This was sufficient to prove Theorem 4.1. Thus, we have shown:

Theorem 4.6

Let φ φ be a partially monotone anisotropy on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}R2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^\varphi $$\end{document}Wφ does not have vertical facets, γ >0γ>0 satisfy (4.18), Λ >0Λ>0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I\subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}I⊂R be a bounded interval. Then every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-local minimizer u of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi $$\end{document}Aφ in I, satisfying the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty $$\end{document}L∞-bound (4.1), is Lipschitz continuous in I. If, additionally, φ φ is elliptic and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^2,$$\end{document}C2, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in C^{1,1}(I).$$\end{document}u∈C1,1(I).

Applications

Minimizers of the perturbed area

Let φ φ be an anisotropy on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^2,$$\end{document}R2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I \subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}I⊂R be a bounded open interval and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in L^\infty (I).$$\end{document}g∈L∞(I). Given p≥ 1,p≥1, consider the functional in (1.7), i.e.,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathscr {G}(u):=\mathscr {A}_\varphi (u,I) + \int _I|u-g|^pds,\quad u\in L^1(I), $$\end{document}G(u):=Aφ(u,I)+∫I|u-g|pds,u∈L1(I),where we set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}(u)=+\infty $$\end{document}G(u)=+∞ if u∉ BV(I)u∉BV(I).

Lemma 5.1

There exists a minimizer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^1(I)$$\end{document}u∈L1(I) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}.$$\end{document}G. Moreover, u∈ BV(I)u∈BV(I) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u\Vert _\infty \le \Vert g\Vert _\infty .$$\end{document}‖u‖∞≤‖g‖∞. Finally, if p>1,p>1, then u is unique.

If p=1,p=1, then in general minimizers are not unique, see the Introduction.

Proof

The proof is standard and we provide it for completeness. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_k) \subset L^1(I)$$\end{document}(uk)⊂L1(I) be a minimizing sequence. We may assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}(u_k) \le \mathscr {G}(0)$$\end{document}G(uk)≤G(0) for all k, therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u_k,I)\le \mathscr {G}(0)\quad \text {and}\quad \int _I | u_k -g|^pds\le \mathscr {G}(0). $$\end{document}Aφ(uk,I)≤G(0)and∫I|uk-g|pds≤G(0).By the convexity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi ^o$$\end{document}φo and (2.1) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathscr {G}(0)\ge \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u_k,I) \ge \int _I \varphi ^o(Du_k) - \varphi ^o(\textbf{e}_2)|I| \ge c \int _I |Du_k| - \varphi ^o(\textbf{e}_2)|I|. $$\end{document}G(0)≥Aφ(uk,I)≥∫Iφo(Duk)-φo(e2)|I|≥c∫I|Duk|-φo(e2)|I|.Moreover, by the Hölder inequality,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _I|u_k|ds & \le \int _I|u_k-g|ds+\Vert g\Vert _\infty |I| \le \Big (\int _I|u_k-g|^pds\Big )^{1/p} |I|^{1-\frac{1}{p}} + \Vert g\Vert _\infty |I|\\ & \le (\mathscr {G}(0))^{1/p} \vert I\vert ^{1-\frac{1}{p}} +\Vert g\Vert _\infty |I|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫I|uk|ds≤∫I|uk-g|ds+‖g‖∞|I|≤(∫I|uk-g|pds)1/p|I|1-1p+‖g‖∞|I|≤(G(0))1/p|I|1-1p+‖g‖∞|I|.Thus, the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_k)_k$$\end{document}(uk)k is bounded in BV(I) and by the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1$$\end{document}L1-compactness in BV, up to a subsequence, not relabeled, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k\rightarrow u$$\end{document}uk→u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(I)$$\end{document}L1(I) for some u∈ BV(I).u∈BV(I). By the Riesz-Fischer lemma, we may also assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k\rightarrow u$$\end{document}uk→u a.e. in I. Then by the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\textrm{loc}}^1(I)$$\end{document}Lloc1(I)-lower semicontinuity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi (\cdot ,I),$$\end{document}Aφ(·,I),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \liminf _{k\rightarrow +\infty } \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u_k,I) \ge \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u,I) $$\end{document}lim infk→+∞Aφ(uk,I)≥Aφ(u,I)and by the Fatou’s lemma\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \liminf _{k\rightarrow +\infty } \int _I|u_k-g|^pds\ge \int _I|u-g|^pds. $$\end{document}lim infk→+∞∫I|uk-g|pds≥∫I|u-g|pds.Therefore, u ∈ BV(I)u∈BV(I) is a minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}.$$\end{document}G. In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in L^\infty (I)$$\end{document}u∈L∞(I).

To show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u\Vert _\infty \le \Vert g\Vert _\infty ,$$\end{document}‖u‖∞≤‖g‖∞, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ v:=\max \{u,-\Vert g\Vert _\infty \}. $$\end{document}v:=max{u,-‖g‖∞}. Since |u-g| ≥ |v-g||u-g|≥|v-g| a.e. in I, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \int _I|u-g|^pds\ge \int _I|v-g|^pds$$\end{document}∫I|u-g|pds≥∫I|v-g|pdswith the strict inequality if the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{u<-\Vert g\Vert _\infty \}$$\end{document}{u<-‖g‖∞} has positive measure. Moreover, by Lemma 2.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \int _I\varphi ^o(-Du,1) = P_\varphi (\textrm{sg}(u),I\times \mathbb {R}) =P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(u),I\times \mathbb {R}). $$\end{document}∫Iφo(-Du,1)=Pφ(sg(u),I×R)=Pφ(epi(u),I×R).Observe that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(v),I\times \mathbb {R}) = P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(u) \cap [I\times (-\Vert g\Vert _\infty ,+\infty )],I\times \mathbb {R}) \le P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(u),I\times \mathbb {R}), $$\end{document}Pφ(epi(v),I×R)=Pφ(epi(u)∩[I×(-‖g‖∞,+∞)],I×R)≤Pφ(epi(u),I×R),where in the last inequality we used a cutting with half-spaces argument, see e.g. [4]. Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u,I) = P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(u),I\times \mathbb {R}) \ge P_{\varphi }(\textrm{epi}(v),I\times \mathbb {R}) \ge \mathscr {A}_\varphi (v,I). $$\end{document}Aφ(u,I)=Pφ(epi(u),I×R)≥Pφ(epi(v),I×R)≥Aφ(v,I).Thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}(u)\ge \mathscr {G}(v)$$\end{document}G(u)≥G(v) with the strict inequality if the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{u<-\Vert g\Vert _\infty \}$$\end{document}{u<-‖g‖∞} has positive measure. Then the minimality of u implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\ge -\Vert g\Vert _\infty $$\end{document}u≥-‖g‖∞ a.e. in I. Similarly, we can show \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\le \Vert g\Vert _\infty $$\end{document}u≤‖g‖∞ a.e. in I.

Let us check the uniqueness of minimizers when p>1.p>1. Consider any minimizers u,v ∈ BV(I)u,v∈BV(I) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in (0,1).$$\end{document}α∈(0,1). We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathscr {A}_\varphi (\alpha u + (1-\alpha )v, I)\\&= \sup _{h=(h_1,h_2) \in C_c^1(I; W^\varphi )} \Big (\alpha \int _I (uh_1' + h_2) ds+ (1 - \alpha )\int _I (vh_1' + h_2)ds\Big ) \\&\le \alpha \sup _{h=(h_1,h_2) \in C_c^1(I; W^\varphi )} \int _I (uh_1' + h_2)ds+ (1 - \alpha ) \sup _{h=(h_1,h_2) \in C_c^1(I; W^\varphi )} \int _I (vh_1' + h_2)ds\\&= \alpha \mathscr {A}_\varphi (u, I) + (1 - \alpha ) \mathscr {A}_\varphi (v, I). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Aφ(αu+(1-α)v,I)=suph=(h1,h2)∈Cc1(I;Wφ)(α∫I(uh1′+h2)ds+(1-α)∫I(vh1′+h2)ds)≤αsuph=(h1,h2)∈Cc1(I;Wφ)∫I(uh1′+h2)ds+(1-α)suph=(h1,h2)∈Cc1(I;Wφ)∫I(vh1′+h2)ds=αAφ(u,I)+(1-α)Aφ(v,I).Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi $$\end{document}Aφ is convex. Since p>1,p>1, the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^p$$\end{document}Lp-norm is strictly convex, and hence, in case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in (0,1)$$\end{document}α∈(0,1) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{u\ne v\}$$\end{document}{u≠v} has positive measure, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathscr {G}(\alpha u + (1-\alpha )v) < \alpha \mathscr {G}(u) + (1-\alpha ) \mathscr {G}(v) = \min \, \mathscr {G}, $$\end{document}G(αu+(1-α)v)<αG(u)+(1-α)G(v)=minG,a contradiction. This shows u = vu=v a.e. on I, i.e., the minimizer is unique. □ □

Remark 5.2

In fact,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ -\Vert g^-\Vert _\infty \le -\Vert u^-\Vert _\infty \le \Vert u^+\Vert _\infty \le \Vert g^+\Vert _\infty , $$\end{document}-‖g-‖∞≤-‖u-‖∞≤‖u+‖∞≤‖g+‖∞,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a^\pm =\max \{\pm a,0\}$$\end{document}a±=max{±a,0} are the positive and negative parts of a.

The next proposition establishes a bridge between minimizers of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}$$\end{document}G and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-minimizers of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi .$$\end{document}Aφ.

Proposition 5.3

Assume that φ φ is partially monotone and let u∈ BV(I)u∈BV(I) be a minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}.$$\end{document}G. Then u is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\gamma ,\Lambda )$$\end{document}(γ,Λ)-minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi $$\end{document}Aφ in I for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma >2\Vert g\Vert _\infty ,$$\end{document}γ>2‖g‖∞, where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Lambda :=p(\gamma + \Vert g\Vert _\infty )^{p-1}. $$\end{document}Λ:=p(γ+‖g‖∞)p-1.

Proof

By Lemma 5.1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u\Vert _\infty \le \Vert g\Vert _\infty .$$\end{document}‖u‖∞≤‖g‖∞. From Proposition 5.3 and Theorem 4.6 we deduce the following

Theorem 5.4

(Regularity of minimizers) Let φ φ be a partially monotone anisotropy on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}R2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^\varphi $$\end{document}Wφ does not have vertical facets, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I\subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}I⊂R be a bounded open interval and p≥ 1.p≥1. Let5.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sigma : = \Big ( \tfrac{1}{4^{p-1}p}\,\min \Big \{\tfrac{\alpha _0 \varphi (\textbf{e}_1)}{4},\, \tfrac{\alpha _0}{2\varphi (\textbf{e}_2)},\, \tfrac{|I|\varphi (\textbf{e}_1)}{4\varphi (\textbf{e}_2)}\Big \}\Big )^{1/p}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ:=(14p-1pmin{α0φ(e1)4,α02φ(e2),|I|φ(e1)4φ(e2)})1/p,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _0>0$$\end{document}α0>0 is defined in (4.2). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in L^\infty (I)$$\end{document}g∈L∞(I) be such that5.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert g\Vert _\infty <\sigma . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖g‖∞<σ.Then every minimizer u of the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}$$\end{document}G in (1.7) is Lipschitz continuous in I. Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma :=3\sigma $$\end{document}γ:=3σ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda :=(\gamma + \sigma )^{p-1}p = (4\sigma )^{p-1}p>0.$$\end{document}Λ:=(γ+σ)p-1p=(4σ)p-1p>0. Moreover, by Lemma 5.1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u\Vert _\infty \le \Vert g\Vert _\infty $$\end{document}‖u‖∞≤‖g‖∞ and hence, from (5.3) and (5.4) it follows that u satisfies (4.1). Now the assertions directly follow from Theorem 4.6. □ □

When φ φ is Euclidean and p=1,p=1, (5.3) reads as5.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sigma = \frac{1}{4}\,\min \Big \{ \alpha _0, |I|\Big \}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ=14min{α0,|I|},where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _0=\frac{2\sqrt{\pi }}{4\pi +1}.$$\end{document}α0=2π4π+1. Thus, Theorem 5.4 implies that every minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}$$\end{document}G belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{1,1}(I)$$\end{document}C1,1(I) provided that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert g\Vert _\infty < \sigma .$$\end{document}‖g‖∞<σ. This gives a positive answer to Conjecture 1.1 in case n=k=1,n=k=1, except for the dependence of σ σ on |I|. The following example shows that in general the local \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{1,1}$$\end{document}C1,1-regularity of u in Theorem 5.4 cannot be improved.

As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g-v \ge g-\bar{v}\ge 0$$\end{document}g-v≥g-v¯≥0 in (0, 1) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v-g \ge \bar{v}-g\ge 0$$\end{document}v-g≥v¯-g≥0 in (-1,0),(-1,0), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \int _{-1}^1 |g-v|ds\ge \int _{-1}^1 |g-\bar{v}|ds$$\end{document}∫-11|g-v|ds≥∫-11|g-v¯|dswith strict inequality if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{v\ne \bar{v}\}$$\end{document}{v≠v¯} has positive Lebesgue measure. Moreover, as we are replacing nonconcave/nonconvex parts of the graph of v with line segments (see Fig. 3b), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {A}_\varphi (v,I) \ge \mathscr {A}_\varphi (\bar{v},I).$$\end{document}Aφ(v,I)≥Aφ(v¯,I). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {G}(\bar{v})\le \mathscr {G}(v).$$\end{document}G(v¯)≤G(v). This inequality shows that we may assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v=\bar{v}.$$\end{document}v=v¯. In this case, by (2.2)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \int _{-1}^1 \frac{u'\,dDv}{\sqrt{1+{u'}^2}} = \int _{-1}^1\frac{u'v'ds}{\sqrt{1+{u'}^2}} + \sqrt{2a-a^2}\, (v^+(0)-v^-(0)). $$\end{document}∫-11u′dDv1+u′2=∫-11u′v′ds1+u′2+2a-a2(v+(0)-v-(0)).Thus, as above, from (5.7), the Hölder inequality and (5.6) we get5.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathscr {G}(u)&= \int _{-1}^1 \sqrt{1+{u'}^2}ds+ \int _{-1}^1 |u-g|ds\nonumber \\&= \int _{-1}^1 \frac{(1+u'v')ds}{\sqrt{1+{u'}^2}}+ \sqrt{2a-a^2}\, (v^+(0)-v^-(0)) +\int _{-1}^1|v-g|ds\nonumber \\&\le \int _{-1}^1 \sqrt{1+{v'}^2}ds+ \sqrt{2a-a^2}\,(v^+(0)-v^-(0)) +\int _{-1}^1|v-g|ds\le \mathscr {G}(v), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}G(u)=∫-111+u′2ds+∫-11|u-g|ds=∫-11(1+u′v′)ds1+u′2+2a-a2(v+(0)-v-(0))+∫-11|v-g|ds≤∫-111+v′2ds+2a-a2(v+(0)-v-(0))+∫-11|v-g|ds≤G(v),where in the last inequality we used \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sqrt{2a-a^2}<1.$$\end{document}2a-a2<1. Since both u and v are minimizers, all inequalities in (5.8) are in fact equalities. In particular, u'=v'u′=v′ a.e. in (-1,1)(-1,1) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v^+(0)=v^-(0).$$\end{document}v+(0)=v-(0). This implies u=v+Cu=v+C for some real constant C. Then, recalling \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u(\pm 1)=\pm a$$\end{document}u(±1)=±a and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-a\le v\le a,$$\end{document}-a≤v≤a, we deduce C=0,C=0, i.e., u=v.u=v.

Notice that Example 5.5 shows that the threshold σ σ in (5.5) is not optimal, in general.
